Local batch import\n\n## Challenge\nManual row entry makes a valid batch cumbersome and a rejected batch must not leave a partially updated destination.\n\n## Goals\nCommit every valid row; if any row is invalid, retain exactly the original destination. Make rejection visible to the user.\n\n## Scope and non-goals\nLocal batch import only. No remote publication, automatic next-stage execution, network service or change to existing single-row behavior. A runtime storage outage and concurrent writers require their own specified behavior before any broader guarantee is claimed; this fixture selects validation rejection.\n\n## Governing principle\nA rejected batch must leave the user where they started.\n\n## Proposed direction\nValidate a complete batch before crossing the persistence boundary. Preserve the all-or-nothing outcome for invalid input; Design owns realization.\n\n## Feasibility\nThe supplied storage interface supports one validated batch write. This is a bounded fixture assumption, not live provider evidence. If that assumption fails, return the constraint and options to the direction owner without weakening the goal.\n\n## Decision requested\nApprove this direction for independently reviewed Design work only. This draft grants no Design approval, implementation, publication or merge authority.\n","sha256:6ab2f34ba602596d88b2a24dfcdd8bcfa772bba65720d5dcf09c5891ce8bde38":"# Importer Design\n\nModel validation contract: model-document-v1\n\n## Introduction and goals\nOwner importer combines child Input validation and child Store persistence. Preserve the approved invalid-batch no-write outcome, with one current owner for each child rule and this composed guarantee.\n\n## Architecture constraints\nDirection is the exact approved proposal; no remote publication is authorized. Input supplies validated rows; Store accepts the complete validated batch. Installation, public release and outage/concurrency guarantees are outside this bounded fixture.\n\n## Architecture overview\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  User[\"Local caller\"] -->|batch request| Importer\n  subgraph ImporterModel[\"Importer model\"]\n    Importer[\"Batch orchestration\"] -->|validate complete batch| Input[\"Input child: row and duplicate rules\"]\n    Input -->|validated batch or rejection| Importer\n    Importer -->|only validated batch| Store\n    Store[\"Store child: validated batch persistence\"]\n  end\n  Store -->|apply validated rows| Destination[\"Existing local destination\"]\n  Importer -->|complete success or validation rejection| User\n```\nThe model owns the orchestration and its two child responsibilities; the caller and destination are external interfaces. Detailed owners are the [context](#context-view), [building blocks](#building-block-view) and [runtime](#runtime-and-interfaces), rather than this summary alone.\n\n| View | Necessity and reason | Owning detail |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Context | Necessary: local caller, accepted direction and destination have distinct external roles. | [Context view](#context-view) |\n| Building Block | Necessary: orchestration, input validation and persistence have different duties and their composition can fail. | [Building Block view](#building-block-view) |\n| Runtime | Necessary: rejection must precede any write. | [Runtime and interfaces](#runtime-and-interfaces) |\n| Deployment | Not necessary: one local process/private destination; no separately deployed service or environment-specific guarantee is selected. | Architecture constraints; adding concurrency or outage behavior requires reassessment. |\n\n## Context view\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  Direction[\"Approved direction owner\"] -.->|invalid input must preserve prior records| ImporterSystem[\"Importer\"]\n  Caller[\"Local caller\"] -->|complete input batch| ImporterSystem\n  ImporterSystem -->|success or specific validation rejection| Caller\n  ImporterSystem -->|validated batch updates only| Destination[\"Existing local destination\"]\n```\nThe caller submits data and receives the actual result; it does not bypass validation. The direction owner defines the supported invalid-input guarantee and remains the recipient of material infeasibility. The local destination begins with independent prior records and may change only after complete validation. Network publication and outage/concurrency semantics are outside the selected contract.\n\n## Building Block view\n```mermaid\nflowchart LR\n  Batch[\"Public batch interface\"] -->|entire request| Orchestrator[\"Importer orchestration\"]\n  Orchestrator -->|all rows and IDs| Validator[\"Input child\"]\n  Validator -->|validated whole batch or defined rejection| Orchestrator\n  Orchestrator -->|one complete validated batch after success| Writer[\"Store child\"]\n  Writer -->|apply rows| LocalRecords[\"Local destination\"]\n  Orchestrator -->|actual completion or rejection| Result[\"Caller result\"]\n```\nInput owns integer-ID, nonempty-value and within-batch duplicate checks under IMPORT-SR-01. Store owns the stipulated local operation accepting one complete validated batch; it does not decide input acceptance or authorize partial imports. Importer owns the invocation order and caller result under IMPORT-SR-02 and IMPORT-DEC-01. Neither child's isolated proof establishes that the public importer waits for complete validation; that interaction needs the real destination observations in C0.\n\n## Requirements\n| ID | Required behavior |\n| --- | --- |\n| IMPORT-SR-01 | Each row has an integer ID and nonempty value; duplicate IDs within the batch reject. |\n| IMPORT-SR-02 | An invalid batch leaves the destination unchanged; a valid batch commits every row. |\n\n### Boundary scan and acceptance scenarios\n| Dimension | Requirement basis | Distinct outcome to demonstrate |\n| --- | --- | --- |\n| Input domain | IMPORT-SR-01 | Integer/nonempty/duplicate distinctions yield exact acceptance or rejection. |\n| State/lifecycle | IMPORT-SR-02 | Invalid input preserves original rows; valid batch adds all rows. |\n| Identity/authority | - | Not applicable: local input has no principal/authorization rule in this bounded model. |\n| Composition/path | IMPORT-SR-02 | Full validation precedes Store invocation; per-row validation alone cannot prove this. |\n| Temporal/retry | - | Not applicable: retries and concurrent writers are explicitly outside this fixture's supported scope. |\n| Failure/recovery | IMPORT-SR-01, IMPORT-SR-02 | Invalid middle row gives nonzero rejection and no destination mutation. |\n| Compatibility/migration | - | Not applicable: no persisted-format migration or retired operation is introduced. |\n| External/environment | IMPORT-SR-02 | Observe the real local destination; no network dependency is claimed. |\n\n## Runtime and interfaces\nThe importer validates all rows and duplicate IDs before invoking Store once. If the middle row is invalid, it returns a validation failure without invoking Store. The valid batch write adds all three rows to the existing destination. Children do not own the parent's sequencing guarantee.\n```mermaid\nsequenceDiagram\n  User->>Importer: batch\n  Importer->>Input: all rows\n  alt invalid row\n    Input-->>Importer: rejection\n    Importer-->>User: nonzero validation failure\n  else all valid\n    Input-->>Importer: validated complete batch\n    Importer->>Store: write complete batch\n    Importer-->>User: complete success\n  end\n```\n\n## Test design\nSee [owned coverage](test-design/cases.md). IMPORT-TC-01/02 retain conditions, independent observations and proposed realization here, independently of a Delivery plan. Parent owns the Input-to-Store no-write interaction; per-row checks cannot observe it.\n\n## Decisions and risk\nIMPORT-DEC-01 chooses complete validation before write over a validate/write loop: the latter can leave row1 behind after row2 fails. Preserve that alternative and consequence. Unknown protected behavior returns to Design; material infeasibility returns to the direction owner.\n\n## Supporting examples\n[Example E0](examples/rejection.md) is an explicitly declared illustration of IMPORT-SR-02, not a workflow record or executed test.\n\n## Limits and handoff\nExact model, coverage and relied-on example go to independent Design Review. Proposed tests are unexecuted. No authored document or save receipt supplies approval.\n","sha256:f2d744cd3b7631c1e119855cb87f6d7544f68364fb5f40a5bfe75a349632dbd9":"# Importer coverage C0\nOwner importer. Requirements IMPORT-SR-01 and IMPORT-SR-02. Proposed realization only; no executed result.\n\nIMPORT-TC-01: Fresh private destination contains exactly [(9, retained)]. Import [(1, alpha), (2, beta), (3, gamma)] through the public batch interface. Observe complete success and exactly [(9, retained), (1, alpha), (2, beta), (3, gamma)]. Literal expected rows are independent of the importer.\n\nIMPORT-TC-02: Establish the valid setup above, then use a fresh destination with the same original row and change only the middle value to empty. Invoke the same public boundary. Observe the specific empty-value rejection, nonzero status, and complete destination exactly [(9, retained)]. Keep real persistence and compare the whole destination; parser-only checks miss a partial row1 write. Each case owns mutable state and cleanup.\n\nR-old: A supported unusual but valid value such as 'alpha beta' remains protected by an existing regression even though it has no separate scenario row. Preserve until independent assessment establishes equivalent detection or the behavior owner authorizes retirement.\n","sha256:dc4679617f54425c1011d86201be0a411ba2c37a27f511b4beae21bb66837e81":"# Coverage index\n\n[Cases](cases.md) belongs to [importer](../importer.md).\n","sha256:58bbb95dc5f54a49ddce6a0ab4968516edd7ef57539b5d57bbdf0760f92e02fb":"# Rejection example E0\n\nDeclared excerpt, not a complete record. Neighbor metadata remains owner=local.\n","sha256:f81bb69f260bd537ce88845630074fc91ca7e504c8e5b0578deb2d400ca40ede":"# System parent\n\n[Importer](importer/importer.md) owns the combined validation-to-write contract; children alone do not establish rejection without mutation.\n","sha256:e13b5a9c683d734b85dc380b353a77fbff823d48ea67b5961ec31ed39a8ac457":"# Batch import delivery plan\n\n## Purpose / big picture\n\nDeliver the approved local batch-import behavior without weakening invalid-batch destination preservation. This is stable synthetic allocation; no command, current review result or milestone completion is claimed.\n\n## Current Handoff Summary\n\n- Owning change record: docs/changes/batch-import/change.json (stable synthetic pointer supplied by authority.md; this packet contains no runtime record and claims no record-schema conformance)\n\nMutable lifecycle state, current milestone state, review status, blockers, routing, and closeout readiness live only in this record.\n\n## Source artifacts\n\n- Proposal: [Direction](proposal.md)\n- Spec: [Design D0](importer/importer.md); living model, no legacy feature output\n- Architecture: [Importer architecture](importer/importer.md#architecture-overview)\n- Prior-contract test spec: none; model-owned [C0 coverage](importer/test-design/cases.md) supplies intent, with no companion test-spec\n\n## Context and orientation\n\n[Design D0](importer/importer.md) and [coverage C0](importer/test-design/cases.md) own the behavior and observations. Importer combines Input validation and Store persistence; [(9, retained)] is the original private destination. D0/C0 assessments and command availability are expressly fictional supplied facts. [Authority](authority.md) bounds actions; history and neighbor.txt remain preserved.\n\n## Non-goals\n\n- No network, remote publication, actual command execution, automatic continuation, storage-outage/concurrency guarantee or change to single-row behavior.\n\n## Requirements covered\n\n- IMPORT-SR-01: M1 and TG-1 input decisions.\n- IMPORT-SR-02: M2 and TG-2 actual success/rejection destination observations; TG-FINAL-1 checks their composed path.\n- R-old: preserve the existing unusual-valid-value regression; catalog absence alone does not permit removal.\n\n## Milestones\n\n### M1. Input validation\n\n- Milestone kind: implementation\n- Engineering purpose: Establish the complete input decision before any persistence connection.\n- Requirements: IMPORT-SR-01\n- Architecture responsibility: Input child; importer owns later invocation order under IMPORT-DEC-01.\n- Dependencies:\n  - Current proposal and exact D0/C0 have the separately stipulated independent assessments; this allocation grants no implementation permission.\n- Implementation scope: Implement row and duplicate-ID checks only; retain existing single-row behavior and introduce no persistence writes.\n- Files/components likely touched:\n  - Input validation component and local partition tests.\n- Required verification:\n  - TG-1 — Integer IDs, nonempty values and duplicate IDs select the intended acceptance/rejection; valid rows remain accepted.\n- Evidence expectations: Literal valid/invalid rows, intended diagnostics, exact subject identities and actual outcomes when executed.\n- Implementation steps:\n  - Establish the valid three-row control, introduce each defining invalid condition in a fresh input, prove expected failure when feasible, implement the rule and rerun the same proof.\n- Validation commands:\n  - row-check (stipulated project-supported command label; no invocation is performed by this packet).\n- Expected observable result: All three valid rows pass; each malformed row or duplicate-ID batch produces its intended rejection.\n- Completion criteria: Every allocated input partition has independent expected observations and current passing proof; no intended behavior remains unresolved.\n- Required evidence: Input/source/test identities, actual row-check observations and limitations in implementation-owned evidence.\n- Review handoff: Independent milestone Code Review of the Input change and its concrete assertions before dependent connection work.\n- Optional commit boundary: `M1: Input validation`\n- Risks:\n  - A broken input fixture can masquerade as rejection protection.\n- Rollback/recovery:\n  - Restore the local Input slice if a valid control or protected partition fails; preserve unrelated user data and do not begin M2.\n\n### M2. A verification-group non-applicability rationale does not waive it.\n\n## Change-level verification\n\n### TG-FINAL-1. Preserve complete batch behavior across Input and Store\n\n- Covers: IMPORT-SR-01/02, M1/M2, public import-to-Store path and retained R-old protection.\n- Demonstrate: Invoke the same public boundary on valid rows and a fresh invalid-middle-row variation. Compare the entire destination to independently specified rows; an implementation that leaves (1, alpha) after rejection must fail. Preserve the unusual valid value and expose actual diagnostics.\n- Evidence expectations: Actual project-supported batch proof with private real persistence, exact subjects/commands/inputs/results and independent Code Review; this packet supplies no execution results.\n- Non-applicability: Not claimed: the composed public path remains required even if local child checks pass. Required final whole-change Code Review is a separate checkpoint.\n\n## Validation plan\n\n- row-check: input partitions and local rejection meaning.\n- old-batch-check: IMPORT-TC-01/02 on the real public/private-destination boundary with complete independent before/after rows, plus R-old.\n- Independent artifact/implementation review: inspect scope, exact subjects, sequencing and whether actual assertions detect partial persistence; no phrase check substitutes for judgment.\n- Command labels are stipulated fixture inputs, not repository commands to execute; confirm actual project support before implementation.\n\n## Risks and recovery\n\n- Risk: Per-row writes or a parser-only replacement can hide partial persistence. Mutable results embedded in this plan would create false authority.\n  - Recovery: Revert the affected connection and its fixtures together, preserve M1 and unrelated user records, and return missing behavior/allocation to the responsible owner. Keep observed results outside stable intent.\n\n## Dependencies\n\n- Current exact proposal/Design authority precedes Plan reliance; M1 proof/review precedes M2; all implementation/corrections precede fresh whole-change Code Review and distinct final Verify. self.assertEqual(import_batch([{\"id\":1,\"value\":\"alpha beta\"}], destination), (0,\"imported\"))\n        self.assertEqual(destination, [(9,\"retained\"),(1,\"alpha beta\")])\n\nif __name__ ==    seen.add(row[\"id\"])\n    return None\n\ndef import_batch(rows, store):\n    seen = set()\n    for row in rows:\n        error = row_error(row, seen)\n        if error:\n            return 1, error\n        store.append((row[\"id\"], row[\"value\"]))\n    return 0, \"imported\"\n","sha256:e3b0c44298fc1c149afbf4c8996fb92427ae41e4649b934ca495991b7852b855":"","sha256:0211997ada3e2b300fd5ca0ba91e29924c3b9f71b309e5833591bc3290262b09":"[{\"id\":1,\"value\":\"alpha\"},{\"id\":2,\"value\":\"\"},{\"id\":3,\"value\":\"gamma\"}]\n","sha256:9dfc4efb81430fe54ef0b795536e328e7ab1af53299180d101d0f1e39e051cd3":"[[9,\"retained\"]]\n","sha256:4ab412939cec2c254118eef84a6ecf017d0ad075439ed8e11ed0d9d5a02974b9":"#
